Verified against the original code: `"#test #testa #testb"` → `" a b"`, exactly the reported symptom.\n2. **Tag text used as a raw regex** — the tag grammar is `^#{1}[^\\p{Z}]+` (any non-space chars), so tags may contain regex metacharacters. `"#c++ #cpp".replaceAll("#c++", "")` → `"++ pp"`, and a tag like `#a(` would throw `PatternSyntaxException`.\n\n## The fix\n\nRemoval now goes through a helper that builds a safe, delimiter-aware pattern:\n\n- `Pattern.quote(tag.getText())` — the tag is matched **literally**, never as a regex.\n- `(?<![^\\s])` / `(?![^\\s])` — the match must be bounded by whitespace or the text boundaries, so only a *whole* tag is removed. (Fixed-width lookbehind, so it\'s portable across regex engines.)\n- One delimiting space (space/tab only, never a line separator) is consumed along with the tag so no doubled spaces or merged lines are left behind.\n- Null guards on title/tag text, replacing the previous latent NPE.\n\nBoth call sites (`DetailFragment.tagNote()` and `ListFragment`\'s bulk tagging) go through `removeTag()`, so both are fixed.\n\n## Verification\n\n- `./gradlew clean assembleDebug` → **BUILD SUCCESSFUL**\n- Added two regression tests to `TagsHelperTest` (`removesOnlyWholeTagsFromNote` for the exact bug-report scenario, `removesTagsContainingRegexCharacters` for the metacharacter case).
